  - **The world answers three questions** (`World::bearers_in`, `ripe_on`, `pick`): the trees and plants of a chunk that bear something, what each bears now, and a picking, at most what is left. Gathering as an action that lasts, the brain that gathers and the report's yearly balance come in the next pull requests of lot 2.
  - **Gathering lasts** (lot 2's second pull request, 16 November 2026): `Intention::Gather` sets a body at work beside a tree or a plant within reach (2.5 m), and the work takes its kind's grams an hour (`per_hour_g`, an hour being a 24th of the game day) a pinch of 10 g at a time, into one item carried, made when the work began, until the plant is bare, the body carries all it can, or another intention (or sleep, or a walk) stops it. Each start and stop is an event; the stop tells the grams. Only the brains gather yet: the protocol does not change. **Storage**: snapshot format **v13**, a body's work (absent before: idle).
  - **Food spoils** its content's `keeps_days` after it was made (D16): refused to whoever would eat it from then, and taken away, wherever it is, when the next day starts, with an event telling what spoiled. A player is told spoiled food as food refused (`not_food`).
  - **Thirst comes faster in the heat and at work** (`needs.ron`): by season (`thirst_heat_percent`: summer 140 %, winter 80 %) and while walking or working (`thirst_effort_percent`, 150 %).
  - **The brain gathers** (lot 2's third pull request, 16 November 2026): it remembers the trees and plants bearing food it saw (256, the nearest to where it last looked: a tribe whose water is far from its camp roams far from it); hungry, it eats what it carries (what spoils first), else the food it saw, else gathers from the nearest it knows bearing now; by day, carrying less than three days of food (30,000 kJ), it gathers a store. A plant it worked bare is left for that crop's year; a walk to one that stops short leaves the plant for the year and its whole chunk for the day, or it would try every bush beyond the same river one after the other. It knows the seasons: it goes looking for food by day, and only when something is ripe somewhere. The brains place a chunk's bearers once and share them (a two-year run of two tribes of five went from over eleven minutes to under three).
  - **Hunger is on, as a gauge** (`needs.ron`: 100 points a day, a point for 100 kJ): it rises and meals lower it, but a full hunger neither kills nor stops the body healing (`starving_per_day: 0`) until the fat reserve of lot 3, so that deaths do not blur the first balances. *(Since D46, 17 November 2026, `needs.ron` has `starving_per_day: 100`: the reserve spent, the body dies of hunger; 20 November 2026.)* The vivarium's report gives each tribe's food year by year: carried at the start and the end, gathered, eaten, spoiled, and the person-days at full hunger.
  - **The valley's food, season by season** (lot 2's fourth pull request, 16 November 2026): `nourriture.md`'s list taken whole for the trees and plants the world places (nineteen crops then, forty-six on 20 November 2026, and the snapshot format v15: spring leaves, summer fruits, peas and grain, autumn's apples, haws, hips and mushrooms, cattail roots all year, haws and hips on the branch through the winter), except what hides an effect (D30): acorns, elder, plantain, fern, field mushroom, parasol and the deadly ones bear nothing in this repository.
  - **A chunk's bearers are held** with its trees and rocks (`ground::Ground`): a gathering asks which plant it is at each start, and a meadow holds thousands; no man changes a tree yet, so they are held by chunk alone (felling will place a chunk's and its neighbours' again).
  - **A brain looks again only some steps away** (`LOOK_STEP`, 8 tiles), for water and for plants: a lake shore holds thousands of tiles of water. **Stopped short of the water far from its camp, it goes back to the camp first**, where its way to the water starts, and forgets the place only when it fails from the camp. **Hemmed in**, a leg of looking around that went nowhere is not followed by another at once: it waits for the next change.
  - **Amended on 18 November 2026: what is there ripens and falls** (D48's "a load, not three states"). What a plant offers on a day, to `ripe_on` and to `pick`, is no longer its whole year's crop from `from` to `until`: it is that crop times its season's curve (`crops.ron`'s `ripen`, none at `from`, the whole on its plateau, none at `until`), less what was picked this year. Gathering early in a season finds less; what was picked early is not taken again from the rest, which keeps ripening. A brain does not mark a plant spent while its crop still ripens (`crops::ripening`), or one gathering in the first days would give the bush up for the year. Nothing more is stored.
  - **Amended on 19 November 2026: a refused gather is left alone an hour, a full load gathers nothing** (the audit of 18 November 2026, F1). Since a plant still ripening is not marked spent, a brain whose gather the world refused (nothing ripe on it yet, or its load full) asked the same gather again at the next tick, for ever: 3 million refusals a game day for 68 people, 80 to 95 % of the tick, and the people stood still (843 picks and 344 meals in their first day). Now **any refused gather leaves that plant alone for a game hour** (`brain::RETRY_HOURS`, `KnownPlant::not_before`; since D53 the plant memory is of places, and the refused plant is held by its key, `Mind::left_alone`): the body goes to another meanwhile, and tries again later, when more has ripened. And a body **chooses to gather only while its load is not full** (the world's own refusal, checked first). Reason: a refusal is news about the plant; asking again unchanged can only be refused again.
  - **Amended on 21 November 2026: a plant is worth what is left on it, and a hungry body sleeps at night** (the point zero of the sim phase; the project lead's go of the same day). The D47 trial no longer held (audit of 20 November, F4): the whole tribe gathered some 4,100 kJ a person-day against 8,000 of need, lived on its fat reserve, and the one with the greatest need (a woman nursing an infant) starved on day 19. Two causes in the brain. (1) It ranked a plant by what an hour of a **full** one gives (`per_hour_g`), so with the fruit ripening along its season (the 18 November amendment) a meadow of thousands of plants holding a trickle each sent it from crumb to crumb: one to thirty grams a gather, thousands of "bare" stops a person, 400 to 2,000 kJ an hour of gathering. Now `plant_to_gather` asks the world what is **left** on each plant it knows (`World::ripe_of`, nothing allocated), takes what an hour of that gives, and **leaves alone a plant holding less than ten minutes of work** (`brain::GATHER_MIN_MINUTES`). (2) Hunger came before the night: a hungry body never slept and walked twenty-two hours a day for nothing. Now a hungry body still eats what it carries at any hour, but **by night it gathers only when its reserve is weak** (`Condition::is_weak`, D46); otherwise it goes home and sleeps like the others. Measured on the D47 trial (30 days, cadence 1, release): 0 dead instead of 2, thin person-days 58 and 30 → 0 and 26, thoughts a person 25,000–40,000 → 1,300–2,300, bare stops 3,000–10,000 → 200–600, food gathered +10 %, the run 34 s → 15 s. The people range farther from their camps (up to 1,350 tiles instead of 650) and come home at night: the scarcity stays, the waste goes. Reason: the project lead keeps hunger as the engine of the sim ("c'est la faim, la soif, le danger… qui font avancer les choses"); what is removed is a body wasting its day, not the difficulty.
  - **Amended on 19 November 2026: the brains' work spread over ticks** (the audit of 18 November 2026, F3: the first tick took 0.4 to 2.6 s, a nightfall up to 1.6 s, and later ticks up to 213 ms). **The dawn and the nightfall** make each mind think in its own tick, within a game minute after (`WAKE_SPREAD_SECONDS`, the body's id modulo the minute's ticks), and so do **a mind's first thought** after it is adopted and **a gauge past a threshold** (a tribe born at once grew hungry in one tick: 150 thoughts at midday of the first day, 110 ms for 170 people). **A camp's trees and plants in sight** (and those around the body) are placed when its people are adopted, not at their first thought. **New land is placed at most ten chunks a game second** (`COLD_CHUNKS_PER_SECOND`, one a tick at the game's ten), nearest first; the chunks left over are placed at the next turns and the body learns their plants then, without a thought. A second rather than a tick, so that what a body knows does not depend on the cadence. Reason: one chunk's plants cost 15 to 30 ms to place on the vivarium's land, and a body seeing new land sees some twenty-five chunks; everything stays deterministic (a body's id and the tick decide, never a clock). **The plants the brains placed are held for at most as many chunks as the world's trees** (`BEARER_CHUNKS`, 1,764), the least lately used forgotten first and placed again if seen again (the audit's F5: they grew with the land the people explored).
  - **Amended on 19 November 2026: a budget of thought a tick** (step 4 of the lightening, the minds thinker's report of 19 November 2026, A1). The brains think at most **`THOUGHT_WORK_PER_SECOND` (100,000) a game second, spread evenly over its ticks** (10,000 a tick at the game's ten), counted in **work, never in time** (D24): a thought counts one, and each chunk its look reads for trees and plants one more (a look over new land reads some twenty-five chunks and costs some ten plain thoughts). The minds due past a turn's share **wait for the next turn, still due**, in turn by their bodies' ids from the first one that waited, so none waits for ever; the turn's last thought runs whole and what it spent past the share is repaid by the next turn, as the path searches' work (D32). The decisions are given back in the bodies' order. **Today it changes nothing**: 800 people of two tribes asked at most 6,377 in a tick of the game's ten in their first day, 7,978 in a tick of a second; at 16, 68, 170 and 800 people no mind ever waited, and the same seeds give the same events, decisions, deaths, picks, meals and positions with it as without it. **Past it** (several thousand minds at a dawn, a tribe meeting new land, a battle later), a mind due reacts a tick or a few later (0.1 s each at the game's ten): what it decides does not change, only when, and from the world as it is then; the thinking a tick costs stays bounded (some 50 ms at the full share, a work costing some 5 µs) instead of growing with the crowd. Reason: the slowest tick must not grow with the population ("slow the thinking, never cheat"); a count, unlike a clock, keeps every run the same. The thought itself was made four to eight times cheaper just before (the places to drink of a chunk found once for all the minds, the kinds bearing food numbered and what they bear worked out once a tick; the same decisions, measured equal), which is what makes the share large enough for today's crowds.
  - **Amended on 22 November 2026: a pause is not a stop, and a body collapsed of fatigue stays down** (the thirst probe `soif-tribu-A-2026-11-22.md` and the brief `BRIEF-fix-thirst-home.md`, both in `content/ai-reserve/review/vivarium/allegement/`; the project lead's go of 22 November 2026, "je suis d'accord avec toutes tes propositions"). Two defects killed tribe A of thirst with its water 354 tiles from the camp. **The false stop**: beyond its sight a body walks straight and stops 30 cm short of each trunk on its line, its goal kept, to look again at the next tick (D32); the world tells that stop as any other (`BodyStopped`, so that whoever saw it set off stops it too), and the brain read it as the end of the walk, "stopped short far from the camp: back to the camp first", 241 times in 25 days, every one with a goal still set and a way that reached the water. Corrected **on the brain's side**, the protocol unchanged: `Body::is_pausing` names a stop with a goal kept short of its target, and the brain ignores such a stop (neither a thought nor a "stopped"); `BodyStopped` keeps its wire meaning for the client. **The collapse**: a parched body does not sleep (the rule above stays), so at full fatigue the world laid it down where it stood, its pilot's next order woke it, it walked a tick and collapsed again, 8,000 to 11,000 decisions in a day and a half and death standing. Corrected **on the brain's side** too: a sleeping body whose fatigue is at `COLLAPSED` (90) or more is asked nothing, even parched, until it has slept a tenth of its gauge off (48 game minutes), then thirst drives it on; the world's rules (collapse at 100, wake on any deliberate act) do not change, a refusal would have needed a reason the protocol has not. The rule "back to the camp first" itself stays, now fired only by a real stop (`BodyBlocked`, `MoveRefused`, or a stop with no goal left); whether it should stay is the lead's (b1 of the probe).
- **Amended on 24 November 2026 — a real year's yield, the world's richness, the cycle's days** (the calendar v1, D09). A crop's `grams` in `content/crops.ron` are what one plant bears in a lean, usual or rich **year of the Earth** (365 days). In the game a plant bears per cycle of seasons **grams × richness × days of the cycle ÷ 365** (`sim::crops::cycle_grams`), so a longer or shorter cycle never makes the land richer or poorer per day. The **richness** is one dial of the world, in content: `richness_permille` at the top of `content/crops.ron`, a D30 placeholder, **7,300** (7.3), which keeps a game day's food what it was with the 50-day cycle (7.3 × 50 ÷ 365 = 1: a legacy world bears exactly as before); the project lead fixes it after measuring. A crop file without the dial (the tests' small crops) bears its grams per cycle as written. The load a plant shows and the days a told state is believed (`fire.told_days_per_hour`) read the cycle's grams.

### D46 — A body lives on a fat reserve; hunger is its appetite
- **Reason**: lot 3 of the vivarium (`PLAN-v3.md`; the values decided by the project lead on 16 November 2026): hunger must stop being a cliff. A body without food grows thin, then weak, and dies after days, as people do; and a day's eating has a limit, so a spent reserve is not made again in one meal.
- **Consequences**:
  - **A body burns its need out of a fat reserve** (`needs.ron`: 10,000 kJ a day out of 60,000): what it eats goes back in, at most 150 % of its need a day. Under half its reserve it is **thin**, under a fifth **weak** (it walks and gathers at 70 % of its pace); the reserve spent, it loses its vitality in a day and dies of hunger. Without food: thin on the third day, weak near the fifth, dead on the seventh.
  - **Hunger is the appetite**: what the reserve misses that can still be eaten today, a point for 100 kJ. A body that ate its day's ceiling has no appetite until the next day, and is refused more (`Refusal::Sated`, told to a player as `unknown`: the protocol does not change). The client is told the same gauges.
  - **Storage**: snapshot format **v14**, a body's reserve (what it misses) and what it ate today; a world saved before has every reserve full.
  - **The brain weighs what an hour of gathering gives** against the walk (halved at 32 tiles), to choose where to gather and which plants to remember: the hazels came back. What bears now is remembered first, then the rest by its best crop (16 November 2026, with 19 more shrubs and plants: fruit bushes out of season, worth more than the spring's leaves by their best crop, crowded the lindens and pines out of tribe B's memory, and the tribe starved in its first spring).
  - **The spring is rich** (the project lead, 16 November 2026: the renewal, not a season of hunger; death will come from predators and men): leaves gathered by the armful, cattail roots and pine inner bark. The vivarium's report shows the reserve by tribe, the person-days thin and weak, and the season of each death.
  - **Amended on 22 November 2026: the reserve is 300 MJ** (`needs.ron`, `reserve_kj: 300000`; validated by the project lead the same day with D50). A grown adult's fat is 10 to 15 kg, 300 to 500 MJ: six to eight weeks without food, not six days. Without food a body is now thin on the sixteenth day, weak on the twenty-fifth, its reserve spent on the thirty-first, dead the day after; thin and weak stay in percent of the reserve, and the day's ceiling (150 %) makes a spent reserve sixty days to make again. Reason: with 60 MJ the vivarium's tribes starved within their first lean weeks (trial 2 of lot 3 C: two tribes of 125 dead in their first year), before any trial could be judged; the real reserve gives the brains the weeks the world takes to judge them (D50), and keeps hunger as the engine. The tests that assumed six days keep their meaning with the old value set in their own content.


### D47 — Ages and sexes; the infant is carried and nursed
- **Reason**: lot 3 B of the vivarium (`PLAN-v3.md`; the values decided by the project lead on 17 November 2026): a tribe is not five grown men. Needs go with age and sex, children are slower and weaker, and an infant is carried and fed by a woman, so that a tribe lives with its children before births come (lot 3 C).
- **Consequences**:
  - **A body has a birth day and a sex**; its age is counted in game years (50 days). Its **size** against a grown man sets its need, its fat reserve, its day's ceiling and what it carries (`needs.ron`, `ages`): an infant 30 %, a child 40 % at two to 90 % at fourteen, a man 100 %, past fifty-five 85 %; a woman 85 % of a man of her age, from fourteen. Children under twelve and the old past fifty-five go at **70 % of the pace**, walking and gathering.
  - **Hunger is counted in points of the body's own need** (amending D46's "a point for 100 kJ" to a point for 100 kJ of a grown man's day): a child of three missed a day before it was hungry enough to eat (its ceiling was some 60 points), and the children died of hunger beside fed adults.
  - **The infant** (under two) is carried: it stands where its carrier stands, does not walk, gather nor act (its intentions are dropped, a walk refused), and has no brain. Carried by a woman whose reserve is not spent, it is **nursed**: fed, watered, as warm and rested as she is, burning nothing of its own, while she needs 25 % more. It weighs 3.5 kg at birth to 12 kg at two on her walk and her load. **Weaned at two**, it is set down and walks and gathers for itself, at a child's pace (food sharing will have its own lot). A dead carrier's infant is taken by the nearest grown woman within 64 tiles who is not carried herself; with none, it is left where she died and soon dies.
  - **A soul cannot pilot an infant** (the project lead, 17 November 2026: to be born a baby may come far later, with powers, luck and ill luck).
  - **Storage**: snapshot format **v15**: a body's birth day and sex, and its carrier. A body saved before is grown, twenty to thirty years old, a woman or a man, both drawn from its id.
  - **Not yet told to clients**: no age, sex or carrier travels in the protocol, and an infant moving with its carrier is not told as a walk; only the vivarium makes infants until births come to the game (the life rule, D49, off in the game).

### D48 — A tree shows its fruit as a load, in eight levels; the server says which
- **Reason**: the project lead's validation of 17 November 2026, "une espèce une image et le masque des fruits": each kind of tree keeps one image, and its fruit is drawn as a layer of it, in **three states chosen the same day: plein, un tiers, vide** (laden, a third, none). The fruit seen on a tree should be what a body could gather from it (D45), so the server, which alone knows the crops and the picks, decides the state; the client only draws it (D03, D05). **18 November 2026**: the fruit is drawn as sprites over the bare image, not as whole images per state. The first build (#267, #268) shipped two more full images per tree for a third and laden, about 2.3 MB more on disk and 5 MB more on the card per tree, multiplied again by every size of tree to come; the project lead asked for the lightest way ("un équilibre entre joli, varié et léger") and validated sprites on the wild pear.
- **Consequences**:
  - **The rule** (`sim::crops::fruit_on`): a kind of tree may declare in `species.ron` a **fruit window** (`fruit`: from and until, a season and hundredths into it, as a crop's, running into the next year when it ends before it starts; a window into the next year is shut in the world's first spring, as a crop is). Out of its window, or without one, a tree shows **none**. In it, when a crop of its kind (`crops.ron`) is in season, what is left on the tree of this year's crop is weighed against what **this tree** bears in a usual year (its share of the kind's usual grams): two thirds of it or more, **laden**; less but something, **a third**; nothing, **none**. A lean year shows a third from the start, a rich one laden; picking lowers it. In its window with no crop of its kind in season, it is **laden**: fruit no one gathers (acorns, cones, yew arils; D45 keeps acorns out of the public crops on purpose, D30), and a private crop for the oak then drives its fruit with no special case. The windows today are placeholders: oak and hazel autumn to winter, yew autumn to 80 % of it, pine autumn to the middle of winter.
  - **Nothing is stored**: the state follows from the date, the seed and the picks already kept (D45). The storage does not change.
  - **Protocol v26**: each tree of a chunk frame carries a byte more, its fruit (0 none, 1 a third, 2 laden), after its species (`StandingTree`).
  - **Sent again when it changes**: the server works the fruit out as a frame is made. A frame is cached by its chunk's versions and by the **fruit phase** (`sim::crops::fruit_phase`: which fruit windows and tree crops are open today, and the crop's year), so it is the same bytes for every client until a window or a crop opens or shuts. A connection notes the phase of each frame its client holds; **at each dawn**, the chunks in view whose phase moved, or whose version moved past what the client was told (a crop picked: the simulation tells the network with `Event::CropPicked`, which keeps the picks beside the items' versions), are sent again, so a client sees the new fruit within a game day; on any other day nothing goes. A chunk back in view from the client's cache goes as `chunk_same` only if its phase is today's; the chunks a reconnecting client kept (`hello`'s `held`) are taken at their version, their phase not known, and sent again at the next dawn.
  - **The fruit drawn**: a kind's image stays bare. Its fruit is a few small sprites of whole fruit, side by side in one texture, `art/trees/fruit/<id>.webp` (the wild pear's four: 53 KB); each tree image has its points, where a fruit hangs, in `art/trees/fruit/fruit.json` (on the lower edge of a leaf, where outlines meet, never on a branch; `content/tools/tree_fruit.py` finds them). A laden tree hangs a sprite at every point, a tree a third laden at the first third of them (ordered so they spread over the crown); which sprite and which way it faces follow from the tree's place. A sprite hangs from the middle of its top edge, the stalk's end under the leaf's outline. The fruit is drawn in the tree's own canvas item, in one call after it, and moves with the crown at its height (`trees.gdshader`, its own marker). A kind without sprites is drawn bare. The same sprites serve every image of the kind, whatever its size.
- **Amended on 18 November 2026: the shrubs too** (the project lead's decision of 18 November 2026). The berry shrubs show their fruit as the trees do, in the same three states and by the same rule, so that what is seen on a bush is what a body could gather from it (D45): their images become bare and their berries a layer over them, so a picked bramble empties on screen and a shrub out of its season is green. What extends:
  - **The rule**: a kind of `flora.ron` may declare a fruit window (`fruit`, the same type and syntax as `species.ron`'s). Fourteen do, each matching the season of its berry crop in `crops.ron`: bramble, dog rose, hawthorn, raspberry, wild strawberry, bilberry, blackcurrant, red currant, gooseberry, blackthorn, cornelian cherry, strawberry tree and wild grape; and the elder, which bears nothing in this repository (D30), from late summer (60 %) to 40 % into autumn, laden through it as the acorns are. A shrub is known by its place (`Source::Plant`, D45), so its picks count as a tree's; `sim::crops::plant_fruit_on` gives its state, `World::plant_fruit_on` the same for a plant of the world. The fruit phase covers the shrubs' windows and crops, so a chunk is sent again when a berry window or crop opens or shuts, as for the trees.
  - **Protocol v27**: each plant of a chunk frame carries a byte more, its fruit (0 none, 1 a third, 2 laden), after its kind (`Standing`); none for a kind without a window. The frames of the valley grow by a byte a plant (1,234,476 → 1,338,344 bytes for its 576 chunks). The client reads them as `plant_fruit`, beside `fruit` for the trees.
  - **The berries drawn**: as a tree's fruit, a few sprites per kind, hung at points of each image of the flora's atlas in `art/flora/fruit.json` (per `<id>_<nn>`, its size and its points in its pixels, the first third spread over the bush; per kind, its sprites' rectangles). **The sprites are in the flora's atlas** (amended on 19 November 2026, audit F3; first a sheet per kind, `art/flora/fruit/<id>.webp`): drawn from a texture of their own, a shrub's berries broke the plants' batch twice per shrub, up to 5,500 more draw calls and 17 ms of render CPU in a laden thicket; each kind's sprites are now one block of the atlas, put where it is clear on a 64-px grid and 32 px from any plant (`content/tools/flora_art.py`, `place_berries`), so the picture is the same and a chunk's plants and berries are one batch. A tree's fruit keeps its own sheet: it is drawn in the tree's canvas item, one call after it. A sprite hangs from the middle of its top edge at its point, at its plant's scale, so the sprite's texels and the plant's are one size; which sprite and which way it faces follow from the plant's place. The low shrubs' berries are drawn on the chunk's plant layer right after their plant (worked out on the worker with the plant); the tall ones' in their plant's own canvas item, sorted with the trees and the bodies. A kind without its data is drawn bare: the data and the sheets are read once, with the flora's atlas, and kept.
- **Amended on 18 November 2026, later the same day: a load, not three states** (the project lead's call for "un modèle simple et qui marche"). Three states said too little: a bush ripening, a rich year and a usual one, a tree half picked, all looked the same. The fruit shown is now a **load**, for trees and shrubs alike:
  - **A season curve** (`crops.ron`'s `ripen: (rise, fall)`, hundredths of the crop's span, rise + fall at most 100; absent, 20 and 30): none at the crop's start, rising evenly to the whole over the first `rise`, the whole on the plateau, falling evenly to none at its end. A crop there all year (cattail roots, wild onions) neither rises nor falls (0 and 0). A fruit window with no crop in season (acorns, cones, the elder) follows the same default curve over its window.
  - **What is there is what can be picked**: on any day a plant offers what it bears that year times its curve, less what was picked this year, never below none (D45, amended the same day): the fruit seen and what a body gathers are one amount.
  - **The load**: what is there now against **this plant's rich-year crop** (its share of the kind's rich grams), at its peak; a rich year on its plateau is full, a usual one shows its usual grams against its rich ones (the hazel's 5 kg against 10, a half; the bramble's 1.5 against 2, three quarters). Quantised to **8 levels** (`crops::LEVELS`): 0 none to 8 full, rounded to the nearest, at least 1 while anything is left. Without a crop in season, 8 times the curve. Out of the window, 0. `crops::Fruit` (none, a third, laden) is gone.
  - **Protocol v28**: the fruit byte of each tree and each plant carries the level, 0 to 8 (`FRUIT_LEVELS`); the layout is v27's.
  - **Sent again when it moves**: the fruit phase hashes, for each kind with a window, whether it is open and then each of its crops in season (its year and its curve as one of the 8 levels), or the window's own curve as a level. With the repository's content and calendar, the phase moves on **39 of the year's 50 dawns** (`the_fruit_phase_moves_on_few_dawns_a_year`); on those dawns the chunks in view are sent again, as since this decision's first build. A load scaled under the curve (a usual year) can step between two of them; it is right again at the next.
  - **The fruit drawn**: a tree or a shrub hangs round(points × level / 8) of its image's fruit points, the first ones (ordered so any first ones spread over the crown or the bush).
- **Amended on 19 November 2026: the fruit alone at dawn** (audit of 18 November, network F1 and server F4; a remedy the project lead approved without a decision of his). Sending the whole view again at each dawn whose phase moved cost **880.6 KB a client a dawn** (361 frames) on 39 of the 50 dawns, 44 % of them byte-identical and the rest differing only in their fruit bytes, and froze the client for 130 to 610 ms each time, as each frame was handled as a new chunk. What changes:
  - **Protocol v29, `chunk_fruit`**: when a chunk the client holds is looked at again and its version did not move (only its fruit can differ), the server sends its trees' and plants' levels alone, one digit each in the order of the frame (`{"type":"chunk_fruit","x","y","version","trees":"0083…","plants":"…"}`, about 200 bytes a chunk), and **nothing** when the levels are those the client shows. A connection keeps, beside each frame's phase, a signature of the fruit it shows (`fruit_signature`, a hash of the levels read from the frame, `protocol::frame_fruit`), in view and in its mirror of the client's cache. A chunk whose version moved (a crop picked) still goes as its frame. The frames themselves, their cache and the phase are unchanged: the server still makes each chunk's frame of the new phase once (shared by every client), and reads its levels from it.
  - **The client** puts the levels in the frame it keeps (`Protocol.with_fruit`), so a later `chunk_same` shows them; a frame it does not hold at that version, or with other counts of trees and plants, is answered `chunk_missing` and comes whole. It redraws only what shows fruit, in place: the trees' canvas items whose level moved (`Trees.Stand.set_fruit`), the tall plants' (`PlantStand.set_fruit`), and the chunk's plant layer, a chunk built at a time nearest the camera first within 1 ms of a frame; never a ground, a neighbour or the far view. A chunk not built has only its data changed.
  - The picture is the one a new frame drew. **Measured** (19 November 2026, headless client on the valley, `--time-scale 1440`, 5 runs a side alternated, about 50 moving dawns a side): **880.6 KB → 57.7 KB a dawn** (median; 21.9 to 62.8 KB; nothing goes for the chunks whose levels did not move), and the client's worst frame at a moving dawn **85 ms (20 to 698) → 20.5 ms (12 to 164)**, the higher ones only in runs where the machine's load passed 10.

### D49 — Births, background deaths and kinship
- **Reason**: lot 3 C of the vivarium (`content/ai-reserve/review/vivarium/lot-3/proposition-3C.md`, validated by the project lead on 21 November 2026): a tribe must renew itself and die of more than want. Women conceive, carry and give birth, people die of illness and of old age, and every death has its place in a family (mother, father, spouse, children), so that the vivarium can hold a population twenty years and report it by family (lot 3 C, PR 2). The rule is one and the same for the game, but it is **off there** (D47 already said that only the vivarium makes infants): the players' bodies do not die of illness at random, and births come to the game with their own decision.
- **Consequences**:
  - **Two clocks**: what is counted in years follows the season (fertility, a pregnancy of so many seasons, the mating years, the death curve by age); what is counted in days follows the day (the daily death draw, the day a child is due, the daily look for a spouse). Changing the length of the year changes neither the deaths a year nor the number of children a life.
  - **Conception** (`needs.ron`, `life`): one draw a season, at `SeasonStarted`, for every woman of the fertile years (16 to 42) with more than 80 % of her reserve, carrying no infant (nursing is the spacing: no counter) and no unborn child, whose spouse is alive within 64 tiles; 40 % a season. Deterministic from the world's seed, her id, the year and the season (D24: no random state beyond the seed; the salts `LIFE_*` in `gen::scatter`). She carries three seasons at 110 % of her need (`pregnant_percent`), then D47's nursing at 125 % takes over; a weak woman (D46, under a fifth of her reserve) loses the child, `Event::PregnancyLost` for the chronicle.
  - **Birth** at the due day's dawn, where the mother stands, the infant carried by her (`World::carry`): 3.5 kg, nursed, weaned at two, as D47. Boys 51 %, from the seed, the mother's id and the day. The mother dies in childbirth with 1 % (cause `Childbirth`); her infant goes to the nearest woman as a dead carrier's does (D47). `Event::BodyBorn { body, mother, father, at }`.
  - **Background deaths**: a curve in per mille a year by age (`death_by_age`: 150 in the first year, 50 to four, 10 to thirty-nine, 20 to fifty-four, 50 to sixty-nine, 150 from seventy, straight lines between the points as `need_by_age`), drawn **every day at an hour of the body's own** (from its id), with the year's chance divided by the days of the year: the deaths of a day are spread over it, not stacked at dawn. Cause `Illness` under `old_age_from_years` (60), `OldAge` from it. Accidents will come with the predators.
  - **Kinship on the body**: `mother`, `father`, `spouse` (ids, never reused) and `pregnant` (`Pregnancy { since_day, father }`). Children are not stored: they are the bodies that have this one as mother or father. A dead body leaves the world (`World::die`) and the world keeps no dead (D41: the chronicle is not a store of the world); its id stays on its living children as their parent, its spouse is widowed.
  - **The spouse**: at 16, or once widowed, a body without a spouse takes, at its own daily hour, the nearest body of the other sex within 64 tiles, aged 16 to 50, without a spouse, that is neither its parent, its child, nor its sibling or half-sibling (same mother or same father). `Event::Coupled { a, b }`. Mechanical and provisional: love (`03`) and society (V4) will replace it. No family id in `sim`: the vivarium's report works families out.
  - **Off in the game**: a world setting, `World::life`, false by default and in every world saved before; `World::set_life(true)` turns it on, and only the vivarium calls it. Off, nobody conceives, is born, couples or dies of illness or old age, and a pregnancy weighs nothing; the scenario tests check it.
  - **Content, not code**: every value is a D30 placeholder in `content/needs.ron`'s `life` section, validated (ages rising, shares at most 100 %, a positive pregnancy).
  - **Storage**: snapshot format **v16**: the body's four fields, the world's switch. A world saved before has no parent, no spouse, no pregnancy and the rule off.
  - **Amended on 23 November 2026 (lot 5b, PR 1 bis) — the season's first dawns.** The project lead, on the causes of PR 1 bis ("je suis d'accord avec tout"): a night apart lost a whole season. The draw stays one a season (the seed, her id, the year, the season), but it is met at the dawn of each of the season's first `conceive_nights` days (4, D30, `needs.ron`): a wife whose spouse is back within `mate_within_tiles` on any of them conceives with the season's chance; one already with child is not drawn again. Sleeping out stays a way of living. Shown before the build: with sleeping out cut, births +10 % (PR 1 bis's control, four seeds); measured after in D56. `GameTime::day_of_season` is new; storage and protocol unchanged.
  - **Amended on 24 November 2026 — a couple apart too long parts.** The project lead, 24 November 2026: couples must form and unmake over time (dynasties over centuries); a couple apart long enough parts, both become single again, free to take another and to have new children; the child stays with one of the two (the mother, as today). At each day's start a body whose spouse stands beyond `mate_within_tiles` counts one more dawn apart (`Body::apart_days`), one back within it counts none; at `part_after_days` dawns in a row (12, about a season, a D30 placeholder in `needs.ron`; 0 never parts) both spouses are cleared, `Event::Parted { a, b }` is told, and each takes another by the daily rule above. The children's mother and father do not change: they stay with their mother. The vivarium's report counts the couples parted and the spouses taken again by tribe and year. **Storage**: snapshot format **v17**, the body's count of dawns apart; a world saved before counts none (no-op migration). Reason: a split at a move called at the fire (D56) left both spouses married at a distance for good, so neither could have children again; a lasting separation now ends the couple, as the lead asked. **Couples form over time, same day.** The forty-year runs of 24 November 2026 (wide map, seeds 1, 2, 3, 9) counted 178 to 259 partings each, as many without moves, and on seed 3, 174 of 244 couples parted after exactly 12 days: the daily rule wed two passers-by of different camps met while gathering, who never slept near each other. The project lead ("c'est un oui"): an unmarried body of the mating years now courts at each dawn — it keeps the one it courts while that one may still wed it and slept within `mate_within_tiles`, else it courts the nearest it may wed there — and the two wed at `court_days` dawns in a row (6, about half a season, a D30 placeholder; `Body::courting`, in the same v17). 0 keeps the rule at once, at the body's hour; the vivarium's departure still pairs its camp at once (`World::couple_all`). **Apart by their homes, same day.** The dawn records of seed 1 without moves (40 years) showed all 139 of its partings were couples sharing one home where one spouse slept 190 to 440 tiles away twelve dawns in a row, the brain's way being to sleep at home; on seed 2, 54 of 87 were the same, the 33 others a spouse whose home moved without the other. The project lead and the thinkers of 24 November 2026: a couple is tied to its home, a spouse sleeping out is not a spouse gone. The brains now tell the world each mind's home before each dawn (`World::set_homes`, the brains' input as intentions are, never persisted), and spouses count apart when their **homes** lie beyond `mate_within_tiles`; a body without a mind counts where it stands. No storage change.
  - **Protocol: unchanged**. A death of `Childbirth`, `Illness` or `OldAge` is told to the client with no cause (`cause: None`, as a witness is told any death; `server::net::wire_cause`); the three causes enter `protocol::DeathCause` the day births enter the game.

### D50 — Trials, selection and imitation: the NPCs invent, without AI
- **Reason**: the project lead, 22 November 2026, after the vivarium's trial 2 (two tribes of 125 starve in their first year on a fixed camp, while two bands of nine fare as their country lets them): "ce qu'on voudrait, c'est des PNJ qui vont évoluer et créer une société… il y a un problème, il faudrait qu'ils trouvent une solution"; and, on an AI to find those solutions: "je suis vraiment pas pour l'IA. Si on peut l'éviter, je suis carrément pour… comment les PNJ peuvent eux-mêmes inventer, construire, trouver des solutions à des problèmes et les mettre en place… construire, en autonomie, des sociétés." The fix for trial 2 (move the camp) was found by the lead and the AI session, not by the brains: the brains had six fixed intentions and no way to try anything else. `docs/03` already says how NPCs discover (a surprise observed, or a trial pushed by a need, one step from what is known; Willpower is the depth of the search; a skill is learnt by watching and dies with those who know it); this decision is how that is built.
- **Consequences**:
  - **No AI in the game, anywhere.** Not in the tick (D24), not beside it. Inventions come from the NPCs' own trials, judged by the world, kept by selection and spread by imitation. Closed in `docs/06`.
  - **The build rule: physics and gestures, never outcomes.** The code holds what a body *can* do (walk, gather, carry, sleep, go home, lead its camp elsewhere; later take, combine, heat, cover, give) and what the world *does* in return (a crop, a night's cold, a death). No rule is named after its result: no "nomad", "tent" or "market" rule enters the code. When fire, shelter or storage come, they come as materials with hidden properties, a few operations and objects defined by what they do (`content/`, D27, the true values private, D30), so that a tribe can find them and another not.
  - **Ways on the body.** The brain's constants that shape a day become **ways**, one set a body, defaulting to today's values: how far from its route a plant is worth going (`WORTH_HALF_TILES`), whether it comes home at night or sleeps where it stands, how much it stores before resting (`STOCK_KJ`), and where home is (`Mind::home`). A way is a value the body carries in its mind, not a rule; the minds live in the vivarium only (`sim::brain` is not driven by the game server yet), so nothing is stored (snapshot unchanged).
  - **A trial pushed by a need.** A body in distress (hungry at the end of the day, or back from a bare day, for `TRIAL_AFTER_DAYS` days running) **tries a way**: one of its ways moved by one step (a search twice as wide, a night out, home set a day's walk towards where it knows the most plants still bearing), drawn deterministically from the world's seed, its id and the day (D24). Its **will** (`will`, a placeholder per body drawn from its id until the souls' Willpower comes, D30) sets how often it tries and how far a step may go: little will, rare and small; much will, often and bold. A trial costs what it costs: a night out in winter can kill.
  - **The world judges.** A trial lasts `TRIAL_DAYS`; then the body compares those days with the ones before (what it gathered a day, its thin days) and **keeps** the way if better, **reverts** if not. Selection then plays three times: on the trial, on the body (it lives or dies of what it tried), on the band (the one holding the better ways grows, the other dwindles, as camps A and B of 22 November).
  - **Amended on 22 November 2026 (D50 b) — the judge counts every distress, and the home step looks at the water too.** Reason: PR #308's runs (two tribes of 25, twenty years: 5 alive with trials against 20 without; most deaths of thirst, some of cold; tribe B's camp drifted 44 times to the map's west edge): a judge that read only the gathered kJ and the thin days kept ways that killed of thirst and cold. The day a mind closes at nightfall now records every distress (gathered kJ, thin, thirsty at `THIRSTY` or more, cold the night before, hungry, no gather), and a trial is **kept only if nothing worsened and one thing improved** over gathered a day, thin days, thirsty days and cold days; reverted otherwise. "Who fared best" for the imitation is the mate with the fewest days of distress over `trial_days`, gathered kJ breaking ties. The home step has **two targets, both perceptions the mind already has**, drawn half and half from the seed: towards where it knows the most plants still bearing (as before), or towards the nearest drinking place it knows (`MEMORY_PLACES`, the camp's water); the other when the drawn one has nothing. No rule "stay near water" (the lead: "ça devrait encore être une décision de la tribu, pas que nous on code"): a home away from water thirsts, the judge reverts it, and the tribe finds "near water" by itself or not. The **map's edge** is physics, not a rule: a direction that leaves the map is not drawn, and a new home lies inside the map less a margin of half the step (the vivarium's world has an edge; the planet will have none, only its ocean). A body whose home can go nowhere tries another way that day. Nothing else tuned (the build rule above: physics and gestures, never outcomes).
  - **Amended on 22 November 2026 (D50 b, the edge) — the home step shortens to the edge less the margin.** Reason: the brain audit of 22 November 2026: a direction whose far end left the map was refused, so with `new_home_walk_tiles: 2000` on the vivarium's 2,176-tile world no adult of will 70 or more ever tried its home (tribe A: 2 home trials in twenty years, 36 to 52 of every other way). The project lead's decision: **the edge is physics, one walks until the land ends**. The step now shortens, along each axis it moves on, to the edge less the margin of half the step; the margin holds on those axes only (where the home already stands near an edge is where it stands, not where it walks); `None`, and another way tried that day, only when the home already stands at or past the margin that way, or when no tile of the shortened line is walkable inside the margin. Content value unchanged.
  - **Imitation.** At night at the camp, a body may copy one way from the camp-mate who fared best over the last `TRIAL_DAYS` (gathered most a day), the more readily the greater that mate's will (prestige), losing a step to the copy at times (a skill degrades, `docs/03`). Home is a way like the others: when the one who fared best has set a new home, those who copy go with it, and the camp has moved, by nobody's rule. Children follow their mother's home; a carried infant its carrier's (D47).
  - **What they can find**: only what the gestures allow, and that is the point. Walking farther, sleeping out, moving the camp come from the first trials; fire, shelter and stores come when their materials and operations exist, found or not found. The gestures grow by lots; the outcomes are never written.
  - **The vivarium shows it**: every trial by its name on the page ("day 212, 14 searches twice as wide, and keeps it"), the ways of each body on its page, counters per tribe and per year (trials, kept, copied, the ways in use), and a control run with trials off (`--no-trials`) beside each trial run. Tiny first: one band of 25 on the vivarium's world, five years, with and without trials; then the two tribes of lot 3 C's trial 2.
  - **Placeholders** (`content/needs.ron`, section `trials`, D30): `trial_after_days`, `trial_days`, the per-will chance and step, the imitation chance and loss, the day's walk for a new home. Every value tuned by reading the vivarium's page, never to remove the difficulty (the lead: hunger, danger and fear are the engine).

  - **Amended on 23 November 2026 (D55) — imitation by hearsay, and a judge that compares with the camp.** The project lead's words of 22 November 2026: "lorsqu'un individu a découvert quelque chose, les autres ne font pas que le copier, ils le recopient parce qu'ils l'ont entendu parler", and "le puits". Two things change in this decision, nothing else. **Imitation**: a body no longer reads its camp-mates' minds to find who fared best (the one place where a mind read another's). At the evening fire (D55) each present tells its way judged that day; a listener holds an access to that record, and copies **only a kept way it heard said** (`Mind::heard_ways`): the teller who fared best during its trial, its credit next, the chance `imitate_percent` scaled by the teller's will as before, the loss of a step as before; the first candidate of a trial in distress is likewise a kept way heard ("I heard it worked for N, I try it"), the judge unchanged. A camp moves because N said at the fire that its moved home did it good, and the others believed it: a few nights slower, and visible. **The judge**: a trial was judged against the body's own five days before, so a week of fine weather or a turning season kept anything, and the twenty-year run kept contrary ways in one tribe (wider and narrower, more store and less, all kept). Now each present tells at the fire how it fared that day (gathered kJ in per cent of its own need, thin, thirsty, cold, and whether it changed its ways lately), and a body in trial sums, night after night, the days of the mates **who changed nothing** (`Trial::heard`); at `trial_days` it compares its own days with their mean (integers, the division whole, [`Lately::better_than`] unchanged: kept if better on one count and worse on none), and falls back on its own days before **only when it heard nobody** (alone at the camp, all in trial, itself out). Same land, same weather, same season: what is common cancels out, the way remains. A perception learnt by listening, never by reading the world; it can be wrong (a teller lucky that day). `News::Tried` says against how many heard days over how many nights, or against itself. The content's `trials` values are unchanged; `--no-fire` (the vivarium) gives trials without any hearsay: nobody copies, every judge is against itself.

  - **Amended on 23 November 2026 (lot 4, PR 1 b) — the will is innate and acquired, and the lessons heard weigh the choice.** The project lead, reading the twenty-year kept ways (every way kept and contradicted in pairs, 128 to 158 trials a way and a tribe): "les essais sont censés arriver avec la volonté… c'est certaines personnes précises qui vont avoir une volonté plus élevée. Et la volonté, elle est à la fois innée, mais aussi acquise… la plupart des gens sont frileux… très peu de gens sont des aventuriers"; and "les leçons d'historien : ça a marché, ça n'a pas marché… ça rentre dans la mémoire collective et les autres arrêtent de le reproduire". Until now the will was drawn flat from 0 to 100 and never moved, and a will of 0 still tried 2 % of its days. **Innate**: the will is drawn from the seed and the body's id as before, but read on a skewed table of percentiles (`trials.will_innate`: the 80 % most timid between 5 and 20, the next between 20 and 40, the last 7 % up to 90: one or two adventurers in twenty-five); a child's the same way, no inheritance (the souls' question, open). **Floor at zero**: `try_percent_by_will` starts at (0, 0), a will of 0 never tries. **Acquired**: a kept trial raises its body's will by `will_step_kept` (5), an abandoned one lowers it by `will_step_lost` (5), within 0 to 100: a trait moved by one's own experience, a gesture, not an outcome. **Lessons**: every trial told at the fire (D55), kept or given up, is held by the listener as an access; when the listener chooses its next trial, each candidate step (wider, narrower, out or home, more store, less store, the home moved) starts at a weight of 100, and each heard trial **of the same change** (the same way, the same direction) adds `lesson_weight` (50) if it was kept and takes it away if it was given up, whole for `lesson_days` (50), half for as long again, nothing after, scaled by how much a body of this will heeds the lessons (`lesson_heed_by_will`: the timid whole at will 0, the adventurer 30 % at will 100), and never under `lesson_floor` (10): **a weight, never a ban**, what failed for one may work for another. The way is then drawn by weight from the seed, the id and the day (`brain::lessons`, `start_trial`); the kept way heard still comes first (D55). `--no-trials` keeps the content's will table (the fire's credit reads the wills) and sets every chance to none. Placeholders in `content/needs.ron`, section `trials` (D30), each explained there.
  - **Amended on 23 November 2026 (lot 5b, PR 1 bis) — the will tempered.** The project lead: the will stays innate, drawn at random, not inherited ("pour l'instant… pas le moment"); the fixed steps (a kept trial +5, an abandoned one −5, about one trial in four kept) wore everyone down: no adventurer left after twenty years. Four rules, each a perception of oneself ("je suis d'accord avec toutes tes solutions"), placeholders in `content/needs.ron`, section `temperance` (D30): (1) **temperance**: failures in a row are counted; the first `bears_by_innate` cost nothing (2 for the most timid, 6 for the boldest: a bold one bears more), each past them costs `failure_milli` (a point) times its rank past them; (2) **a success weighs more the later it comes**: it forgets the streak and gains (n + 1) units for a streak of n, the unit computed from the rarity of a kept trial (`kept_percent`, 25) so that **average luck gains on average what its failures cost** (integer sums, `Temperance::success_milli`); all failures, it loses faith; luck, it asserts itself; (3) **the temperament comes back**: each season the will closes `relax_permille` (83 ‰) of its gap to the innate will, half of it in two years; (4) **being listened to gives confidence**: half a point (`listened_milli`) when another chooses to join its call at the fire (not a child taking its mother's decision) or copies a way it told. The will is kept in thousandths (`Mind.will_rest`); `Mind::will` stays its points. Absent, the section leaves the fixed steps of before (`will_step_kept`, `will_step_lost`), which the vivarium's control and the tests keep. The same four rules hold a confidence by activity: D56, amended the same day. Measured over twenty years (two tribes of 25, seed 1, cadence 1, PR 1's binary and content against PR 1 bis's, one run each on a shared machine): the mean will of all who lived 15.5 at the start, **7.8 at the end with the fixed steps, 18.2 tempered**; a will of 40 or more at the end 2 / 9 (the bold of tribe B 3 in its first year, 3 to 6 in its last five); trials 274 / 493 judged, 64 / 76 kept (23 % / 15 %); alive 63 / 55, dead 30 / 32 (30 of illness each), births 43 / 37; gathered 502.0 / 460.8 million kJ (−8 %); the food carried at noon, the mean of the forty tribe-years, 2,600 / 1,239 thousand kJ (tribe A alike, 1,021 / 1,083 a year; tribe B's halved: more trials, fewer kept, its stock of ways lower). One seed: the ten-seed series says whether it holds.

  - **Amended on 23 November 2026 (lot 5b, PR 1 bis, after its causes) — the slow ways judged on their horizon.** The project lead: "il faut les deux corrections… et garder bien entendu la volonté tempérée", and the slow ways judged over their natural horizon rather than five days. A trial of a way named in `trials.horizon_days` lasts that many days and is judged on as many, its own and the mates' heard (`Trials::days_for`; placeholders: `stock_kj` 12 and `sleep_out` 12 days, a season of the vivarium's fifty-day year); the other ways keep `trial_days` (5), never fewer; a mind keeps twice the longest of them (`Trials::longest_days`). Why: a stock or a night at the camp changes nothing in five days of gathering, so keeping or dropping one was a coin toss, and the tempered will, trying twice as often, tossed twice as many (seed 1 of the causes: tribe B's stock 7,500 against 178,125 kJ in PR 1, and the reverse on the other seeds). Measured in D56.
  - **Amended on 23 November 2026 (lot 5b, PR 1 ter) — the horizon counted in seasons.** The project lead validated it ("je suis tout à fait d'accord, c'est un bel ajout"), then fixed the home: a home tried alone is a short stay, then back if not kept; a season's horizon is for the acts of a group. `trials.horizon_days` becomes `trials.horizon_seasons`, (way, seasons), converted by the calendar (`seasons × days_per_year / 4` in whole days, `Trials::days_for`, `Trials::longest_days`); placeholders `stock_kj` 1 and `sleep_out` 1, twelve days of the vivarium's fifty-day year, the same as before. `home` stays unnamed, so a lone home trial keeps `trial_days` (5). Why: a horizon in days is tied to one year's length; in seasons it follows the calendar, and a longer year lengthens the slow ways with it. The acts of a group do not exist yet: their season comes with them.
  - **Amended on 23 November 2026 (lot 5b, choosing where to live) — home goes where the body lived best.** The project lead: "ils doivent se souvenir des endroits où vivre et choisir eux-mêmes"; "Ce n'est pas quelque chose qu'on veut coder, on veut quelque chose qui réfléchit soi-même. Mais il est absolument essentiel qu'ils trouvent l'endroit optimal où vivre"; and the rule of every trial: "j'ai essayé, ça a marché, je le fais; à force de répéter ça ne marche plus, je change". A home trial no longer steps a day's walk towards the plants: it goes to the place the body itself slept at whose notes are the best (D53, the night's notebook), slept at for at least the nights its will asks (`trials.home_nights_by_will`, three for the timid, one for the bold). With no such place better than its home, it draws another way that day. `trials.new_home_walk_tiles`, `trials.far_home_from_will`, the direction drawn and the edge's margin go. A body hungry **or thirsty** at nightfall now counts a day of distress. The judge, imitation by hearsay (D55) and the fire's decisions (D56) are unchanged. **On 24 November 2026** (the lead: "ajouter les endroits de la journée au carnet"), the first run having shown notebooks holding only the camp where everyone sleeps, the place may also be one it drank or gathered at in its days: "I walk far every day to drink or gather there; what if I lived there?" **Then the same day, the grade made one** (the lead: the home must end at the optimal place, for good, "pas juste les prémices"; no ordered list of criteria): a place is graded by how the body would fare living there, every perception in its own gauge's unit and counted alike, per mille of a day of that need: what its grove gave (food gathered, water drunk, each up to a day's worth) less what its nights left lacking (hunger and thirst at nightfall, cold at dawn) and what walking cost; a place never slept near takes the home's nights, their walk less what living there would save. A new perception (fear, warmth kept, sight) is one more `brain::Sense` and enters the grade by itself. **The bold search for better (24 November 2026**, the lead's idea b): with nothing wrong, a body may still start a trial, by `trials.calm_try_permille_by_will` (none under will 40, 2 % a day at 100), judged the same. **The night's cold follows the season (24 November 2026**, the lead): `needs.cold_season_percent` scales `cold_per_day_at_night` (spring 74, summer 32, autumn 63, winter 100, in proportion to a temperate lowland's mean nightly minimum under about 20 °C), so only winter nights fill the gauge; the world's climate by place is not read yet. **Walking costs energy (24 November 2026**, the lead: "que la marche coûte de l'énergie"): a body pays `needs.walk_j_per_m` (130 J a metre for a grown body, about 2 J per kg per metre above rest, a smaller body by its size) for every metre it walks, on top of the day's need; thirst from effort is unchanged.
  - **Amended on 24 November 2026 (lot 5b, PR 2 a) — one home at a time.** The project lead: "c'est important qu'il ait une notion : maintenant c'est ma maison, ce n'est plus celle-là. C'est là où il va revenir." A body has **one home** (`Ways::home`): the place it returns to at dusk, sleeps at (unless it sleeps out) and whose fire it sits at. A home trial sets the new home at once, provisional until judged: kept, it is the home, and the old one survives only as a memory in the notebook (`Mind.lived`); not kept, the trial's ways before set it back and the body returns. No other home exists anywhere in a mind: `home_at_dusk` is only that home as it was at nightfall, the fire the body lies down for while the dawn's vigil may move the home.
  - **Amended on 24 November 2026 — the innate will from the run's seed.** The project lead ordered it fixed: the innate will is drawn from the run's seed and the body's id (salt `TRIAL_WILL`, the same skewed table), no longer from the map's, which gave every body the same will whatever the vivarium's `--seed`; in the game the world's seed is the map's, so nothing changes there.
  - **Amended on 24 November 2026 — the brain's daily draws from the run's seed too.** The project lead agreed: every draw of the brain (whether to try, the calm trial, the way tried, copying a way and losing a step of it, the look and the reputation at the fire, the credit given to a teller: salts `TRIAL_TRY`, `TRIAL_CALM`, `TRIAL_WAY`, `TRIAL_COPY`, `TRIAL_COPY_WAY`, `TRIAL_COPY_LOSS`, `FIRE_LOOK`, `FIRE_REPUTE`, `FIRE_CREDIT`) now comes from the brain's seed, the run's, no longer from the map's, so two runs on one map live other days. The world's own draws of life (conception, a child's sex, death in childbirth, the daily death) stay on the map's seed: moving them would put a run seed in the world and its snapshot, left for later.
  - **Amended on 24 November 2026 (lot 5b, PR 2 b) — a move is called at the fire, each one decides.** The project lead: no vote, "sur les 20 personnes qui l'écoutent, il y en a 15 qui sont d'accord et qui y vont… plusieurs individualités". A home trial drawn from the body's own notebook (`lived_home`) is no longer moved at nightfall: it is held (`Mind.calling`) and the body lies down at its old camp, so that at the dawn's fire it can say "I go to live at X, who comes?" (`Act::Moves`, D56). Its trial starts then, or alone if it was at no fire. Each one who joins opens **its own** home trial toward X (`Trial.caller` names whom it followed), judged by the world alone like any other trial: each can come back. A home heard at the fire is **no longer copied by `imitate`** (the copy by prestige was the camp moving on someone else's word); a kept home heard still comes first when the body tries (`heard_candidate`), as a trial judged as any. When the body says stop on its way to a home beyond a day (D56, the held goal), the move is given up as a trial is: the home goes back, the will learns it. Reason: the lead's rule that the collective comes from individual decisions, each judged by its own body, never from a group rule; and "one home at a time": home is where it comes back.
  - **Amended on 24 November 2026 (lot 5b, PR 2 b) — a home trial judged by its grade over a season** (the project lead, 24 November 2026: "il faut vraiment que le déménagement apporte quelque chose": the bodies better their standard of living and move only to a place that is really better, bringing over the medium and long term more food, more children, more of everything). A home trial was judged on five days by the strict test (`Lately::better_than`: nothing worse, one thing better), the moving and settling days among them, so it failed almost always (the previous control run: all 16 of a move on day 40 came back after five days, the caller's notes saying +139). Now a home is tried over **a season** (`trials.horizon_seasons` names `home`, one season, 12 days of the vivarium's year, as `stock_kj` and `sleep_out`) and judged by **the same grade that chose it** (`brain::fare`): the new home's, by the body's own nights and days there, against the old home's by its notes (`brain::home_grades`); kept when the new beats the old by the margin (a hundredth of a day). Never slept at the new home, or the same place, given up; no notes left of the old home, it counts nothing. The other ways keep the old judge. The moving day enters the new home's notes as any day: within a day's walk the body sleeps there that night, so the night's cold and company are its first night there, and what it gathered on the way is noted at the groves where it gathered; the notes fade a twentieth a day, so the season outweighs the first days. `News::Tried.grades` tells both grades (`people[].trials[].grades_permille`).
  - **Amended on 24 November 2026 — a move is a consequence, never drawn.** The project lead, 24 November 2026: "Faut pas du tout que ça soit un but. L'idée, c'est une amélioration, une évolution… Le déménagement est une conséquence, pas une cause", and "une personne qui arrive dans un endroit et se dit : ici, c'est tellement mieux, c'est ici qu'il faut venir"; the synthesis of the three thinkers, validated that day (`content/ai-reserve/review/vivarium/lot-5/synthese-penseurs-demenager-2026-11-24.md`, "Le mécanisme proposé", points 1 to 7). **The home leaves the trials**: `Way::Home` is no longer a candidate of `brain::lessons`, nor a way heard (`heard_candidate`) or copied; the will still drives the other trials, the bold search for better and the wish to go and see. **A move is called** (`brain::call_move`, at nightfall, held for the dawn's fire as in PR 2 b) only by a grown body with a place it lived **surprises** at (D53 amended: the days its will asks, `trials.home_nights_by_will`, its spouse's days counting by `moving.spouse_day_percent`) whose grade beats its home's, season against season, by the margin once the move is repaid, **and** that feels **pushed** here (its recent days worse than its usual days of the season by `moving.push_permille`), or whose surprise there was **huge** (`moving.huge_times` the threshold). A home trial is then judged as in PR 2 b, by the same grade, now season against season (`brain::season_grades`). Reason: a move drawn by chance was a search, not an answer; a move must come from what the body lived here and there. Placeholders in `content/needs.ron`, section `moving` (D30); absent, nobody moves home (`--no-moves`, the vivarium's control). Measured (two tribes of 25, five years, seeds 9, 1 and 2, with and without `--no-moves`): alive 82, 83, 72 with moves against 85, 85, 85 without; births 37, 40, 30 against 40, 40, 40; no death of want either way; 16, 20 and 50 homes moved, 4 to 19 % given up after their season, no return A, B, A by a new move within a year; the movers had about as many hungry nights as the stayers over the season after on seed 9 (0.56 against 0.50), fewer on seeds 1 and 2 (1.25 against 1.65, 0.48 against 1.92); the cost was couples apart at the dawns of conception (seed 2: 448 against 37). With these placeholders, moving does not make the population grow faster: left to the project lead.

### D51 — The vivarium runs fast: the world's step costs what moves
- **Reason**: the project lead, 22 November 2026: "on va faire tourner beaucoup de runs… de plus en plus au cerveau… il va falloir des runs beaucoup plus rapides". The runs are the vivarium's judgement (D41, D50): a run of two tribes of 25 over 40 days took about a minute, and the brain's lots to come each ask for pairs of them. Lightening is measured first (`perf`, call stacks on a frame-pointer build), and a change that alters a result is wrong: the results must be the same before and after (D24).
- **Consequences**:
  - **The invariant.** A change is checked by the pair of the vivarium's runs (`--world vivarium --people 15 --children 7 --infants 3 --cadence 1 --seed 1 --tribes 2 --days 40`, with and without `--no-trials`): the `sha256` of each page **with its timing paragraph removed** (since lab 6, 23 November 2026, the long page: `--page full`; the default page is minimal, D41) (`<p id="timing">…</p>`, the one place the wall time is written) must equal the reference's. The hashes of 22 November 2026: `c23a897558b835eb…` (trials) and `5ee8345c42aafd3d…` (no trials), the same before and after this decision's changes, on the release build as on the profiling build.
  - **The terrain is made once a chunk and read in place.** Terrain is still generated from the seed, never stored as tiles (D31, unchanged): `Land` makes a chunk's tiles on first touch and holds them in a bounded cache (`TILE_CACHE_CHUNKS`, 6,596 chunks, about 8 MB: four views with their rim and the far view's chunks; the vivarium's 4,624 chunks fit, the game's valley of 9,216 does not, and its oldest chunks are made again when read, the same bytes). Counted over the vivarium's 40 days: every chunk's tiles made once (4,624, nearly all by the page's picture at the end, which reads the whole map), 1,712 chunks of woods, 1,432 of plants, 1,691 of trees, each once: nothing was made twice. What cost a quarter of the process was the **reading**: a thread kept its four recent chunks in a list searched and reordered at every tile, the chunk cloned out (`Arc`), and a path search in a window of 19 × 19 chunks fell past the four to the land's lock and its cache (two maps updated a read). A thread now keeps a **direct table of 16 × 16 slots** a kind (tiles, woods, water), a chunk's slot its place in that square, so chunks less than sixteen apart never share one; the tile is read in place. Bound: 256 slots × 3 kinds, about **1 MB a thread** at most, pinned past the land's caches. A walk's planning (`TrunkLookup`) likewise keeps the chunk of trunks it read last.
  - **Routes are not planned less.** Counted over 10 days of the same run: 14,362 departures (29 a body a day), 14,285 on a fresh order of the brain, 255 (1.8 %) toward the target already held; 11,172 searches of **37 tiles expanded on average** (budget 1,500). Planning less would change the routes, so the results; the cost was each tile read, now cheap. `Routing::route_from`, 25 % of the first profile, is the continent's water routed at birth (`plan::relief`), not a body's route, and is left as it is.
  - **Measured** (release, the pair alternated with the reference on a loaded machine, so the gain is read within a pair): run **58.9 → 46.1 s** and **67.9 → 49.8 s** (−22 to −27 %), the birth unchanged (13 s); CPU of the whole process 76.8 → 63.8 s and 86.9 → 67.1 s. Per body-tick (288,000 ticks, about 50 bodies): about 4.1 → 3.2 µs. Shares of the process: `Land::terrain` with its recent list 13.7 → 5.8 %, `TrunkLookup::blocks` 3.7 → 2.5 %; `gen::noise` 8.6 → 10.3 % of a shorter whole (the chunks' drafts, once each, mostly for the picture). What remains, measured for the next step: the brain at 38 % (the plants placed as the minds learn them, 19 %: about 6 ms a chunk, already held once; `crops::text_key` hashing a name through a fresh `Vec` at each call, 3 %; the known plants' map, 6 %), the walks' planning at 20 % (`Map::skirt` 4 %, the search itself), the birth at 17 % (the continent, which the server keeps in SQLite, D28, and the `vivarium` binary bears anew).
  - Protocol and storage unchanged; no dependency added; `plan/` untouched.
  - **Amended on 22 November 2026 (lightening 2: the brain, nothing visible changes).** The brain audit of the same day (`content/ai-reserve/review/vivarium/allegement/audit-cerveau-2026-11-22.md`) read the brain at 65 % of the loop after the terrain's lightening, three items making three quarters of it: the plants placed as the minds learn them, the choice of the plant to gather (every known plant re-read in the world by name), and the plants seen again and rejected at every look. The lead agreed to the lot that changes nothing visible, under the same invariant: the pair of 25 a camp (`--people 25 --tribes 2 --days 40 --cadence 1 --seed 1`, with and without `--no-trials`) hashes the same, page without its timing paragraph (`04330094958504db…`, `5b9efeb9013ffd3b…`) and JSON without `meta.timing`, after each of eight commits. **S1** the brain asks what is ripe by the crop's number (`World::ripe_of_crop`) instead of two names compared against the 46 crops, and a kind's name is hashed as its bytes come, no vector (20.7 million calls a run, 50 million vectors). **S2** a look computes once the rank of the plant its full memory keeps last (`memory_bar`) and learns only what outranks it: ranks are total, so the set remembered is the same (1,364 plants a look were pushed and rejected). **S3** the plant to gather is chosen once a thought. **S4** a chunk's bearers are held once, the brain reading the world's own list with two bytes a bearer for its kind's number; a bearer's kind is a shared `Arc<str>` made once a kind a chunk, written as text so the snapshot's JSON is unchanged (D28); the trees around are read in place, not copied. **S5** the due minds are noted during the one sweep that stays (the gauges), and only the minds with chunks left unseen are read for them. **S6** the brains' caches of bearers and shores widen to the world's chunks (`Lru::widen`, the vivarium's 4,624; 1,764 was four views and two tribes of 60 overflowed it on day 19), held once with the world's. **Measured** (release, reference and new alternated on a loaded machine, so read within a pair, CPU time of the process): run CPU of the pair, machine quiet, three alternations agreeing within 0.4 s: **31.9 → 26.5 s** with trials and **31.7 → 26.2 s** without (**−17 %**, the birth's 7–8 s unchanged in both); the loop's two sections by the audit's probe (the same timers on both builds, two runs each agreeing within 0.1 s): `Brains::think` **15.18 → 11.36 s (−25 %)**, `World::step` 8.75 → 7.95 s, the brain's share of the loop 63 → 59 %; inside the brain, the choice of the plant 3.90 → 1.85 s (S1, S1 b, S3), the plants pushed for ranking 54.2 → 9.1 million and `remember` 1.52 → 1.00 s with the learning 2.08 → 1.11 s (S2, S2 b), the sweeps 0.79 → 0.75 s (S5); placing a chunk's bearers 6.24 → 6.09 s (S4: the cost is `flora::plants_in`, intrinsic, left for a later lot or S11); peak RSS 146 → 140 MB (S4 held the bearers once, but they are not where the memory is). Over 250 days on a loaded machine, 313 → 200 s of CPU, the page the same (`9ca080882d1eaf56…`). Two steps were measured first and done again: S1 b hashes a crop's kind once with the content (`World::crop_keys`, not stored) instead of at every read of a plant; S2 b reads the bar before searching the memory and compares worths through products, not quotients (`Bar::admits`, checked against the rank by a test), because the first S2 saved in `remember` what it spent in the learning. S4 keeps `Bearer.kind` a text in the snapshot's JSON (D28): the audit's `u16` would have changed the storage. S6 changes nothing on the pair, which never reached the cap, and changes the pages of two tribes of 60, which did. Not done: the behaviour steps of the audit (S7–S13: deciding before looking, the plant chosen once an hour, patches instead of bushes, hunger hysteresis, a shorter plant sight, the worth's distance), each a rule for the lead.
  - **Amended on 22 November 2026 — a chunk's bearers placed ahead of the bodies, on helper threads** (`sim::ahead`; the lead's yes to the parallel study's alternative (a)). Placing the trees and plants bearing a crop of a chunk (`flora::plants_in` over the trees of nine chunks, a few milliseconds a chunk, once a chunk) was 39 % of the brain at the audit of the day, asked on the simulation thread the moment a body looked at new land. The placing is a pure function of the map, the seed and the content, immutable under a world, and its result is memoised in the world's shared `Ground` where the server's threads already read it (D29). So a `Placer` of `--helpers N` plain `std::thread`s (default 1; 0 is the simulation thread alone, today's path) is told every 16 ticks where the bodies stand, how far they see and where their routes end (`Placer::follow`, a bounded channel the simulation never blocks on), and places first the chunks of each body's sight widened by two chunks (`LEAD_CHUNKS`) and of its route's end, nearest first, each once, skipping what is held. The simulation thread asks the same cache and finds them placed: **the same bearers, placed ahead or placed now, by construction**; a late helper changes nothing but the time, the simulation thread placing the chunk itself as before. The helpers decide nothing, write nothing a rule reads, and the simulation never waits for them: workers of the one simulation thread (D29), not a second simulation. The vivarium also widens the world's ground caches (trees, rocks, bearers; `Ground::widen`) from four views (1,764 chunks) to the world's chunks, as the brain's own were (S6), with or without helpers: the bodies see more land than four views over a long run, the helpers fill the caches with the land around the bodies, and a chunk's bearers read nine chunks' trees. The server keeps its caches as they are (the valley has 9,216 chunks; widening them is about 4 MB of trees, a decision of its own). The lab (`tools/lab`) passes `--helpers 0` when it runs several runs side by side: each helper takes about a fifth of a core more, which a series spends better on another run. Measured on a quiet machine (load about 3, 0 and 1 helper alternated, two passes within 0.1 s), the pair of 25 a camp over 40 days with trials: the loop **32.7 / 31.4 s → 24.7 / 24.8 s (−23 %)**, the simulation thread's own CPU 42.5 / 41.1 → 34.5 / 32.4 s, the process's CPU 43.2 / 41.8 → 51.2 / 49.0 s (+18 %: about a thousand chunks placed ahead that no body looked at, and the helper placing at about 9 ms a chunk beside the simulation); the widened caches alone give −6 %. Over 250 days with trials: **187 → 93 s (−50 %)**, of which the widened caches alone give 187 → 97 s: at 1,764 chunks the trees' and bearers' caches thrashed from day 59 on (5,670 placings for 4,380 chunks explored, each placing its nine chunks' trees again), and the helper adds −4 % on top. Pages and JSON (without the timing paragraph and `meta.timing`) identical with 0, 1 and 2 helpers on the 40-day and 250-day pairs (before the rules of D53: `b8ac93618fff39cb` / `46ddf3150d9bbd31` over 40 days, `d4e0faa12105f69b` / `2e400ed36006eda2` over 250, the values of #315; after them, the 40-day pair with 0 and 1 helper again alike). With D53 a body sees plants no farther than `plant_sight_tiles` (30), and the helpers follow that sight. The timing paragraph and `meta.timing` tell the helpers and the chunks placed, in all and ahead. Not done, for the lead: the server's caches, and a wider lead or a helper pool for the game's server, where a view's chunks are the bodies' to come.
  - **Amended on 22 November 2026 (lightening 4: the world's step, nothing visible changes).** The world's step audit of the same day (`content/ai-reserve/review/vivarium/allegement/audit-pas-du-monde-2026-11-22.md`) read the step at 43.5 % of the loop after lightening 2, routes 76 % of it, and the A* exhausting its budget of 1,500 tiles to say "no way in sight" before a river: 85 % of the tiles expanded, some 40 % of the step. The lead: "à part S12, on peut absolument toutes les faire". The seven steps that change nothing visible, one commit each, the pair of 25 a camp hashing the same after each (page without its timing paragraph `b8ac93618fff39cb…` with trials and `46ddf3150d9bbd31…` without, JSON without `meta.timing` `16f9c04e583a97e2…` and `161c8902b249c082…`, as main's), and 250 days (`d4e0faa12105f69b…`, JSON `a7d16127c1ee5831…`) and the pair with children and infants (`8f2205b11118b18c…`) the same too. **S1** the connectivity pre-test by chunk before an A* (`foule/chemins.md` §2, PLAN A2, never coded): each chunk's walkable pieces (`ground::Links`, a label a tile, four-connected, under the movement rule's terrains and the trunks; 2 KB a chunk, held by the `Ground` per chunk, version and walkable mask) joined across the chunks' edges of the sight square by a union-find (`TrunkLookup::may_join`); a target no piece joins, whole chunks and all, is refused with no tile expanded, the budget counted as spent as a search at its budget is; "maybe" runs the A* as ever.
